| fixed | `libs/asio/test//cancel_at`; the timer-backed `cancel_at_timer` path instantiates dependent trailing-return calls that forward pointer arguments through `static_cast<Args&&>(args)...`, including `T * const &` forms from libc++ invoke/identity machinery during the perf gate. | The structural type-id clone added for forwarding references did not preserve pointer declarators or top-level cv on the pointer itself. After cached semantic types were substituted and cleared, `T * const &` could degrade to `T * &`, causing invalid return checking in libc++ `__invoke` and leaving the Boost.Asio timer path at an `async_wait` member lookup failure. The fix builds pointer type-ids structurally with a `ptr_operator` child, emits `const`/`volatile` cv-qualifier children after `*` for top-level pointer cv, and uses the declaration-model type spelling only as child display text. No source-text reparse path was added. | `pa22:400` | `pa22/tests/general/400-forwarding-pack-pointer-cast-trailing-return.t` | The checked-in reducer isolates forwarding a pointer pack through a trailing-return `static_cast`, including the `int * const &` case. | fixed | `libs/asio/test//cancel_at`; `boost::asio::detail::timed_cancel_op::start` calls `slot.template emplace<cancel_proxy>(this)`, where `cancel_proxy` is a nested type under an owner containing a function-pointer template argument. | Variadic member-function result substitution cleared the freshly substituted structured `Handler=cancel_proxy` type before reparsing the result pattern, so lookup fell back to owner text and resolved the nested type in the wrong scope. The PA23 owner report also exposed a cached `type-id` bug where `(F**)0` reused the specifier `F` semantic type instead of parsing the full abstract declarator, causing incomplete class completion during template-template deduction. | fixed | `libs/asio/test//append`; nested `append(bind_executor(...), 1, 2)` completion tokens instantiate `async_initiate<append_t<...>, void(int)>` while stale fallback overload cache entries from earlier dependent contexts are still present. | A cached no-body function-template instantiation whose pack-dependent trailing return remained dependent was reused as a later concrete overload candidate. No source-text reparsing or fallback resolver is added. | `pa23:500` | `pa23/tests/general/500-async-initiate-cached-sfinae-pack-return.t` | Reducers `/tmp/async_wait_nested_fullshape_with_fallback_probe.cpp` and `scratch/asio_append_async_wait_model.cpp` failed with `ERROR: unknown member function [class waitable_timer<int>] [member async_wait]`; trace showed the bad `async_initiate` fallback surviving via a function-template instantiation cache hit. Focused Boost `libs/asio/test//append` failed in the same completion-token `async_wait` cluster. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused reducers and controls pass; clang accepts the PA23 reducer; focused PA23 regression passes; PA23 template-placement audit reports the reducer as a PA23 `500` integration candidate; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa23' ... make test-report-nobuild` passes `523/523`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes PA18, PA19, PA21, PA22, and PA23 with PA23 `467` compared; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; focused B2 `JOBS=12 ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/asio/test//append` passes and updates 23 targets, log `/tmp/boost-asio-append-after-cached-sfinae-pack-return-20260618-final.log`; perf against `/tmp/cppgm-origin-main-3e469910-20260618-perf-baseline.json` currently misses the cumulative dirty-tree instruction gate (`+1.21%`, RSS `+0.41%`, footprint `+0.12%`, report `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-asio-append-cached-sfinae-perf-report-pack-scoped-runs3.json`), and a temporary guard-disabled measurement produced the same `+1.21%` instruction miss, isolating the miss away from this fix (`/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-asio-append-perf-report-guard-disabled-temporary.json`). | this commit |
| fixed | PA23 parked completion-token/property SFINAE reducers from `libs/asio/test`; member-template owners such as `_TCC<...>::template __is_implicitly_constructible<_Args...>()`, `supportable_properties<I, void(Head, Tail...)>::template is_valid_target<T>`, and member alias templates passed as template-template arguments must resolve from structured syntax. | Parser and template resolution preserved the leaf template-id arguments but not enough structured qualifier template-id payload for member-template owners, so owner lookup could fall back to source text or miss substituted packs. The fix threads qualifier template-id syntax through parsed template-id arguments, resolves member template-template arguments from that syntax, recurses pack substitution through qualifier template-id payloads, and skips type-id resolution for call-expression arguments. | fixed | `libs/asio/test//as_tuple` and `libs/asio/test//as_tuple_select`; `async_result<use_future_t<Allocator>, Result(Args...)>` must preserve `Result(Args...)` as a function type and use the corresponding `promise_async_result<void(decay_t<Args>...), Allocator>` base initializer. | Template-argument syntax kept the expression parse for zero-argument call-shaped type fragments such as `void(tuple<error_code>)` but did not also retain a structural function `type_id` when the type-id parse was unavailable. Parameter-clause pack expansion also looked only at the declarator, so a pack in the function return/type-specifier side was missed. The same batch repaired remaining strict/report regressions where explicit function-template and static-member lookups still bypassed node-aware structured qualifier syntax, dropping owner template-id payloads. The fix builds the empty-function type-id structure from call-expression syntax, expands parameter packs found anywhere in the parameter declaration node, routes qualified function-template lookup through node-aware paths, and resolves out-of-class static-member partial-specialization owners from structured template-id syntax. No source-text reparse path is added. | `pa21:300`; `pa23:400`; `pa23:500` | `pa23/tests/general/500-dependent-function-type-pack-expansion-ctor-init.t`; existing guards `pa21/tests/general/300-nested-class-template-current-owner-lookup.t`, `pa23/tests/general/400-static-data-nttp-pack-sizeof-bound.t`, and `pa23/tests/general/500-dependent-qualified-member-template-result-bool.t` | Focused Boost `as_tuple` and `as_tuple_select` were still in the Asio completion-token frontier after the parked member-template owner fixes. | fixed | `libs/algorithm/test//empty_search_test`; Boost.Test `framework.o` builds `boost::function<void()> log_cleaner = boost::bind(&unit_test_log_t::set_stream, &unit_test_log, format, boost::ref(std::cout))`. | The selected Boost.Bind member-function pointer overload forms a trailing `decltype(boost::bind(boost::mem_fn(f), ...))`. | fixed | `libs/asio/test/execution//context_as`; Boost.Asio query CPO reducers use `decay_t<Property>::template static_query_v<T>` where the qualified member variable-template specialization has class type and static storage. | Structured qualified lookup acquired the variable-template specialization, but the class-scope materialization did not preserve the concrete owner and variable-template instantiation identity all the way to expression/output symbol construction. | fixed | `libs/asio/test/execution//context_as`; `boost::asio::thread_pool::basic_executor_type<std::__1::allocator<void>, 0>` probes `execute(invocable_archetype)` through `decltype`/`noexcept` while the owner class template transitions from reference collection to full collection. | Discarding functions during class-template reset erased the owned `FunctionBinding` but left raw pointers in `FunctionTemplateDecl::instantiations`. An out-of-class member-function template could therefore leave a dangling cached `execute<invocable_archetype>` binding on the primary source template; the next probe reused that stale pointer and segfaulted. The fix erases discarded function bindings from all recorded function-template instantiation caches, using a sparse one-entry-plus-overflow representation so ordinary functions and the common single-cache-entry case avoid unnecessary allocation. No source-text reparse or fallback resolver is added. | `pa23:400` | `pa23/tests/general/400-out-of-class-member-template-cache-reset.t` | With the cache-erasure call temporarily disabled, the checked-in reducer segfaulted with rc `139`, and focused B2 `libs/asio/test/execution//context_as` failed during `context_as.o` compilation. LLDB on the original failure showed `instantiate_function_template` reading a dangling cached binding for `execute`, with `source_template` already freed/corrupted. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; focused PA23 regression passes and refs were generated with `REF_TEST_APP=../dev/cppgm++`; PA23 placement audit exits cleanly; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa23' ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=12 TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `527/527`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 STRICT_SUBTEST_JOBS=12 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-strict-nobuild` passes PA18, PA19, PA21, PA22, and PA23; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; focused B2 `JOBS=12 ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/asio/test/execution//context_as` passes and updates 23 targets, log `/tmp/boost-asio-context-as-cache-entry-small-20260619.log`; perf initially missed with a `unique_ptr<vector>` cache-entry list at `+1.02%` instructions, then passed after the one-entry-plus-overflow optimization against `/tmp/cppgm-origin-main-3e469910-20260618-perf-baseline.json` (`+0.90%` instructions, RSS `+0.65%`, footprint `+0.43%`, log `/tmp/cppgm-stale-function-template-cache-small-perf-20260619.log`). | this commit |
| fixed | PA22/PA35 cleanup after the current tree regressed the supplemental Asio static-query reducer and accumulated an expensive tuple/SFINAE investigation WIP. | Reentrant class-template partial selection returned a temporary primary for `static_query<T, untracked_t>` while another partial-selection path was still deciding `static_query<T, tracked_t>`. That temporary primary exposed `is_valid = false` as a concrete value, so the mutually-recursive Asio-style `can_query` condition selected the wrong partial. The fix marks reentrant/deferred primary selections, avoids caching them, propagates the marker to instantiated `ClassInfo`, and treats bool/static member values from such owners as dependent rather than false. The broad tuple/alias WIP was discarded and saved as `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-expensive-wip-dev.patch`; no source-text reparse or fallback resolver is added. | `pa22:500`; `pa35` hosted compatibility coverage | Existing `pa22/tests/general/500-reentrant-static-query-enable-if-partial.t`; new guards `pa22/tests/general/500-dependent-std-or-enable-if-defers.t`; `pa35/tests/compile/600-hosted-forward-as-tuple-string-include-order.t` | Clean HEAD already passed the new PA22 `_Or`/`enable_if_t` and PA35 `forward_as_tuple` guards, proving the discarded WIP was not needed for those cases. The real regression was the existing PA22 `500-reentrant-static-query-enable-if-partial.t`, which failed with `static_query<executor, untracked_t>::is_valid` evaluating false. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; focused PA22 reentrant reducer passes; focused new PA22 and PA35 guards pass; direct tuple probe `dev/cppgm++ -Idev/src -DPROBE=13 -c scratch/tuple_project_header_probe.cpp` passes; PA22 and PA35 placement audits pass with `--fail-on-early`; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; serialized direct-LowIR report `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa22 pa35' ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=12 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `137/137`; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-origin-main-3e469910-20260618-perf-baseline.json` passes with `--runs 3` (`+0.90%` instructions, RSS `+1.41%`, footprint `+0.45%`, report `/tmp/cppgm-reentrant-static-query-perf-runs3.json`). | this commit |
| fixed | Current Boost B2 revalidation `libs/bimap/test`; first representative failures were `test_mutant_relation`, `test_bimap_ordered`, `test_bimap_convenience_header`, and related targets where `boost::bimaps::relation::detail::mutate` reached `BOOST_MPL_ASSERT((mpl::contains<Type::mutant_views, View>))` as `mpl_::assert<false>`. | MPL `vector` typeof lookup builds inherited static overload sets with `using Base::item_`. Hidden-base overload cleanup compared member-function types by skipping parameter 0 as though it were always an implicit object parameter; static member functions have no implicit object parameter, so `item_(index_<0>)`, `item_(index_<1>)`, etc. looked identical and were removed until the ellipsis fallback won. The fix compares all explicit parameters for static member functions while preserving the non-static object-parameter skip. No source-text reparse or fallback resolver is added. | `pa34:700` | `pa34/tests/compile/700-gnu-typeof-dependent-static-member-call.t` | Reduced Boost.MPL `v_at` over `vector4<C, D, E, F>` selected `vector0::item_(...)` and produced `mpl_::void_`; the earlier PA34 test only covered a one-element vector and lacked a static assertion, so it did not catch inherited static overload hiding. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; focused PA34 regression passes; PA34 placement audit passes with `--fail-on-early`; PA34 direct-LowIR report passes `272/272`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes PA18, PA19, PA21, PA22, and PA23; text-reparse audit reports all zero; focused B2 `libs/bimap/test//test_mutant_relation` passes; full B2 `libs/bimap/test` updates 199 targets with Bimap tests passing and only the separate Boost.Container `global_resource.o` dependency failure remaining; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-origin-main-3e469910-20260618-perf-baseline.json` passes with `--runs 3` (`+0.99%` instructions, RSS `+0.60%`, footprint `+0.54%`, report `/tmp/cppgm-bimap-pa23-placement-perf.json`). | this commit |
| fixed | Existing strict direct-LowIR PA23 reducer `pa23/tests/general/400-member-variable-template-leaf-sfinae.t`; `selected<executor, property<> >` incorrectly selected the primary and returned `1`. | Constant lookup for a qualified id-expression resolved the structured qualifier scope, then checked only ordinary class values. For substituted member variable-template syntax such as `Property::template is_applicable_property_v<T>`, the leaf `TemplateIdSyntax` was never materialized into a variable-template binding in that qualified path, so `enable_if_t<!!Property::template is_applicable_property_v<T>>` instantiated as `enable_if<false, void>`. | fixed | PA39 self-host compile of `dev/src/callsemantic.cpp` and hosted `std::pair<Local *, Local *>` construction with a local class declared inside a lambda. | Non-type bool argument evaluation cloned and substituted libc++'s `__is_nothrow_constructible(_Tp,_Args...)` before builtin type-trait AST evaluation. The fix evaluates builtin type-trait ASTs against the live template bindings before substitution when the result is concrete, preserving structured bound types and packs without adding source-text reparsing or a fallback resolver. | `pa34` hosted compatibility; PA39 self-host implementation compatibility | New `pa34/tests/compile/701-hosted-pair-local-pointer-constructor.t` | Scratch reducer `scratch/pa39_pair_local_ptr_nothrow_reducer.cpp` failed before the fix while direct builtin-trait reducers passed, confirming the loss occurred in template-argument substitution/replay rather than the raw trait implementation. `make -C pa39 test-through-pa10 ...` then advanced past the prior `callsemantic.o` failure. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; focused PA34 pair reducers and PA35 string/pair guard pass; focused PA39 `../obj/pa39/selfhost/shared/release/callsemantic.o` passes; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=4 TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 make test-report CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1` passes `3319/3319`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 make test-strict CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1` passes; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; PA34 placement audit reports `placement findings: 0`; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-clean-head-9f9b8308-perf-baseline-r3.json` passes (`-0.67%` instructions, RSS `-0.12%`, footprint `-0.01%`). PA39 `test-through-pa10` now passes PA1-PA9 and reaches separate PA10 `template_text_output.o` / `template_witness_renderer.o` failures. | this commit |
| fixed | Local direct-LowIR strict/report regressions from the parked Boost frontier alias/default-argument work: member alias templates used as template-template arguments, forward specialization member aliases, and PA23 alias/SFINAE witness stability. | Bound class-template shell types could remain in template scopes after a forward specialization member alias was selected, so later alias uses saw the shell instead of the concrete specialization. Pack expansion through a member alias template, `L<F<T>...>`, resolved `F<T>` to a concrete type but dependency scanning could reparse the expanded type-id and turn a witness scan into a hard substitution failure. Separately, alias/default dependency recomputation emitted nested class/declval witnesses from predicate-only semantic probes. The fix canonicalizes bound shell types through structured template metadata, preserves expanded `TypePtr`s across pack-syntax normalization, makes dependency predicates side-effect-free and conservative on internal substitution failure, and pauses declval capture while parsing partial-specialization pattern syntax. No source-text reparse or fallback resolver is added. | `pa21:200`; existing PA23 alias/SFINAE strict coverage | New `pa21/tests/general/200-alias-template-binds-forward-specialization-member.t`; existing `pa23/tests/general/400-member-alias-template-template-empty-template-id-argument.t`, `300-pack-expanded-enable-if-member-value.t`, `300-recursive-streamable-sfinae-guard.t`, `400-dependent-conditional-default-alias-recomputed.t`, `500-alias-default-dependent-qualifier-type-trait.t`, and `500-pack-alias-inherited-bool-constant.t` | PA23 strict had one compile failure in `L<F<T>...>` and five witness drifts where dependency probes emitted alias internals such as `is_void`, `is_convertible`, `Unique`, `inherited_true`, or `declval` calls. The new PA21 reducer locks the forward-specialization member-alias shell canonicalization. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; focused PA23 reducer set passes or compares cleanly; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 make test-strict CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1` passes PA18, PA19, PA21, PA22, and PA23; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 make test-report CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1` passes `3320/3320`; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; PA21 placement audit has `placement findings: 0`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-pa21` passes `85/85`; `git diff --check` passes; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-clean-head-9f9b8308-perf-baseline-r3.json` passes (`-1.70%` instructions, RSS `-0.36%`, footprint `-0.87%`). | this commit |
| fixed | PA39 self-host PA7 compile of `dev/src/callsemantic/constant_value_lookup.cpp`; libc++ `std::prev(const char *)` checks `__has_bidirectional_iterator_category<_InputIter>::value` before advancing the pointer. | Static-assert analysis accepted the generic constexpr member value before the structured bool fallback could correct libc++ iterator-category alias materialization. The alias path expanded through `is_convertible<__nat, bidirectional_iterator_tag>` even though the live iterator type was `const char *`, whose `iterator_traits` category is random-access. The fix keeps the normal constexpr path first for witness stability, but if it evaluates a static assertion to `false`, it gives the structured bool evaluator one chance to override the result from typed template information. No source-text reparse or fallback resolver is added. | `pa34` hosted compile coverage; PA39 self-host compatibility | New `pa34/tests/compile/700-hosted-prev-pointer-iterator-category.t` | Focused repros for direct `std::__has_bidirectional_iterator_category<const char *>::value`, templated alias-trait use, and public `std::prev(p + 1)` failed before the static-assert override and pass after it. | fixed | u24 clang/libc++ PA35 report failed while compiling `<chrono>` because libc++ `std::__1::forward_list<_Tp, _Alloc>::forward_list(size_type)` could not build an ABI IR symbol for the dependent member-alias parameter. | Dependent class alias placeholders preserved the alias expansion AST, but not the owning class/member identity. In the libc++ `forward_list` case, re-expanding `allocator_traits<allocator_type>::size_type` from the constructor symbol context could not see the class-scope `allocator_type` alias, so symbol-linkage failed before the standalone `abi_mangle` emitter ran. The fix stores `named_member_owner_type`/`named_member_name` on dependent class aliases and has the Type-to-ABI-IR bridge emit the typed owner/member name directly before trying alias AST expansion. No abimangle test was added because the low-level mangler was not the failing layer, and no source-text reparse or fallback resolver is added. | PA35 hosted compile coverage; PA32/PA30 ABI symbol bridge | New `pa35/tests/compile/600-forward-list-dependent-size-type-compile.t`; existing `pa35/tests/compile/600-chrono-duration-convert-owner.t` | CI logged `failed to build ABI IR function symbol for weak function std::__1::forward_list<typename _Tp, typename _Alloc>::forward_list` and `parameter-type-failed ... allocator_traits<allocator_type>::size_type`. A synthetic non-STL PA32 reducer did not fail on the pre-fix compiler, so the checked regression uses the direct hosted `<forward_list>` header that owns the observed failure. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused PA35 forward_list and chrono tests pass locally; u24 clang/libc++ focused PA35 forward_list and chrono tests pass; u24 clang/libc++ `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa35' TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 ... test-report` passes `76/76`; PA35 placement audit reports `placement findings: 0`; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-clean-head-9f9b8308-perf-baseline-r3.json` passes (`-1.19%` instructions, RSS `-1.44%`, footprint `-0.83%`). | this commit |

The defaulted `sizeof(T)` argument then lost the structured `Candidate *` type during constexpr parsing, dropped the true `is_complete_or_unbounded` overload, and left the later `__niter_base` aliases unsupported. The fix carries substituted `TypePtr`s through `type_id` AST nodes and lets type-id parsing consume attached semantic types. No source-text reparse, fallback resolver, or mangler change is added. | `pa34` hosted compatibility; PA39 self-host compatibility | New `pa34/tests/compile/700-libstdcxx-uninitialized-copy-local-aliases.t` | CI/u26 GCC failed PA39 at `semantic_conversion.o` with `unsupported alias-declaration` inside `std::uninitialized_copy`. A tiny reducer showed `template<class T, size_t = sizeof(T)>` calls with a local pointer could become `unknown function`. The standalone ABI mangler was not involved, so no abimangle test was added. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; direct PA34 compile passes; focused PA34 check passes; PA34 placement audit reports `placement findings: 0`; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; `git diff --check` passes; u26 GCC rebuild passes, focused PA34 check passes, and focused PA39 `semantic_conversion.o` passes with the final source; u26 GCC `test-through-pa10` passed PA1 through PA10 while validating the same failing path; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-clean-head-9f9b8308-perf-baseline-r3.json` passes (`-1.53%` instructions, RSS `-0.85%`, footprint `-0.86%`). | this commit |
| fixed | u24 GCC PA39 `test-through-pa10` regression while compiling `dev/src/semantic_class_model.cpp`; libstdc++ checked `std::is_nothrow_default_constructible<_ExtractKey>::value` in a hosted hashtable trait path. | The structured standard-trait shorthand evaluator recognized `std::is_default_constructible`, `std::is_copy_constructible`, and `std::is_move_constructible`, but not the corresponding nothrow shorthand traits. The nothrow forms could fall through to ordinary/cached trait evaluation and either report false or leave later trait bases malformed. No alias-template expansion, source-text reparse, or fallback resolver is added. | `pa34` hosted compatibility; PA39 self-host compatibility | New `pa34/tests/compile/600-hosted-nothrow-default-constructible-shorthand.t` | Exact CI object repro was `~/ralph-ci --no-sync u24-gcc -C pa39 ../obj/pa39/selfhost/shared/release/semantic_class_model.o CXX=../dev/cppgm++ CPPGM_HOST_CXX=g++ -j1`; it failed before the fix with `static_assert unevaluated: is_nothrow_default_constructible<_ExtractKey>::value` and later base-completion diagnostics while probing broader dirty-tree experiments. Reverting the broad alias-template experiment and keeping only the nothrow shorthand fix still passes the exact object repro. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused PA34 reducer passes; PA34 placement audit reports `placement findings: 0` and `local test hygiene findings: 0`; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; exact u24 GCC PA39 `semantic_class_model.o` repro passes; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 ORDERED=false make test-report-nobuild` passes `3327/3327`; direct-LowIR strict passes; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-clean-head-9f9b8308-perf-baseline-r3.json` passes (`-1.44%` instructions, RSS `-1.09%`, footprint `-0.86%`). | this commit |

| fixed | u24 GCC PA39 `test-through-pa10` regression while compiling `dev/src/callsemantic.cpp`; libstdc++ `std::__is_invocable_impl` formed `std::__bool_constant<_Nothrow && !_Dangle>` through a function-template result alias. | Materializing an alias-result class template could prefer stale named/ClassInfo metadata over attached dependent class-template metadata. | fixed | Boost.Asio `libs/asio/test//any_completion_handler`; `std::bind` should deduce through `std::__invoke` when a function-template result type contains a dependent qualified template-id whose first non-type argument is a compact relational expression. | Function-template result parsing retained stale cached semantic types after structured substitution on non-pack result patterns, and qualified template lookup discarded attached `QualifiedName` syntax when normalized display text differed from the lookup text. In `mu_return2<0<is_placeholder<Ti>::value, Ti, Uj>::type`, that let compact expression text confuse the qualifier/template-id match and left the result path unresolved. The fix clears cached semantic types before result-pattern substitution and trusts attached structured qualified syntax when qualifier payloads are present; the component-name fallback only compares the template name prefix and does not reparse template-argument text. | `pa23:300` | `pa23/tests/general/300-dependent-bool-qualified-result-substitution.t` | Non-STL reducer failed the dependent bool qualified result substitution; Boost-header reducer failed with `ERROR: unknown function std::__invoke`, and focused B2 failed while compiling `any_completion_handler.o`. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; no-STL and Boost-header reducers compile; focused PA23 reducer passes; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa23' ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `372/372`; PA23 placement audit reports `placement findings: 0`; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; `git diff --check` passes; focused `/usr/local/bin/timeout 900 env JOBS=8 ... ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/asio/test//any_completion_handler` passes and updates 23 targets, log `/tmp/boost-asio-any-completion-handler-after-dependent-bool-qualified-result-20260630.log`; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-pr36-3ee8fb3f8-20260630-perf-baseline.json` passes (`+0.32%` instructions, RSS `+2.02%`, footprint `+0.02%`). | this commit |
| fixed | Boost.Algorithm `libs/algorithm/test` advanced past the Boost.Bind member-template `call_impl` failure in `index_sequence_for<A...>`. | Alias template instantiation resolved all three arguments to a conditional alias before substituting the alias target. For `if_c<N == 0, identity<...>, if_c<...>>`, resolving the unused branch forced completion of recursive `make_integer_sequence_impl_<T, 0>` and left the enclosing member-template call with no viable `call_impl` candidate. The fix recognizes the structured three-argument conditional-alias target shape and, when the bool condition is concrete, resolves only the selected branch. | fixed | Boost.Asio `libs/asio/test//buffer`; `boost::asio::mutable_buffer` must accept a span-like object whose constructor template has defaulted SFINAE parameters depending on an earlier function parameter. | Instantiated function-template parameter collection parsed the whole parameter clause before binding earlier function parameters, and stored default-argument pointers into a temporary single-parameter AST. The fix instantiates function parameters progressively, binds each parameter before the next one is analyzed, stores default-argument pointers from the original AST, and removes output-time semantic reparsing for parameter aliases/ABI hooks in favor of existing typed bindings plus syntactic names. | fixed | Boost.Asio `libs/asio/test//connect` and `//connect_select`; `connection_sink` starts a `posix_thread` with `std::bind(&connection_sink::run, this)`, then `posix_thread::func<...>::run` invokes the stored bind object through `f_()`. | Structured hosted `std::__is_invocable(_r)_v` evaluation modeled ordinary functions and callable objects but not C++ INVOKE member-pointer forms. libc++ therefore computed `__is_invocable_v<void (sink::*)(), sink *&>` and the top-level-cv pointer-to-member variant as false, selected the primary `__bind_return`, and left `__bind::operator()` with a dependent result type, so overload resolution reported the stored bind object as not callable. The fix evaluates pointer-to-member invocation from typed member-pointer/object arguments, including object pointer/reference, derived owner conversion, member-function cv/ref qualifiers, and data-member result cv. No source-text reparse or fallback resolver is added. | `pa35:700` hosted libc++ bind/invocable compatibility | New `pa35/tests/compile/700-hosted-std-bind-member-pointer-invocable-compile.t` | Direct reducer `/tmp/cppgm_std_bind_direct_min.cpp` failed with `ERROR: callee expression is not callable [type class std::__1::__bind<void (sink::*)(), sink *>]`; trace showed `__is_invocable_v` false for `void (sink::*)(), sink *&` and `void (sink::* const)(), sink *&`. | fixed | Boost.Asio `libs/asio/test//spawn` and `//spawn_select`; `boost::context::detail::invoke` computes `typename std::result_of<Fn&&(Args&&...)>::type` for a nested fiber entry-point callable. | Function-template result substitution could resolve `std::enable_if` but leave the selected `std::result_of<ConcreteCallable &(fiber &&...)>::type` as a dependent qualified member. The later cache-refresh path then accepted that still-dependent substituted result because it no longer mentioned the original template parameter names, overwriting the concrete return type. The fix resolves standard `result_of<Fn(Args...)>::type` through the same typed invocation machinery used by hosted invocability traits, applies the standard meta-member shortcut to carried dependent class-template owners, and prevents cache refresh from replacing a binding with a still-dependent result. No source-text reparse or fallback resolver is added. | `pa35:700` hosted libc++ `std::result_of` compatibility | New `pa35/tests/compile/700-hosted-std-result-of-nested-callable-pack-compile.t` | No-STL reducer `/tmp/cppgm-result-of-boost-shape.cpp` failed first with `unknown function detail::invoke`, then after partial resolution with `call-expression does not return indirect value`; Boost-header reducer `/tmp/cppgm-boost-asio-spawn-exception-min.cpp` failed at `boost::context::detail::invoke`. | fixed | Boost.Asio `libs/asio/test//spawn` and `//spawn_select`; `allocator_binder<std::__bind<spawn_handler<...>, int, double>, allocator<void>>::operator()` computes `std::result_of<T(Args...)>::type` through member-template callable overloads. | Typed invocation-result evaluation did not instantiate callable-object member function templates, and the standard meta-member shortcut did not cover libc++ `std::__invoke_result` / `invoke_result` aliases. After that was fixed, ordinary overload collection still appended declaration-only member-template specializations whose implicit object could not bind, allowing an unused `const&` allocator-binder overload body to be materialized. The fix resolves `result_of`/`invoke_result` through typed member-template `operator()` candidates and filters impossible member-template call candidates by the instantiated implicit-object parameter before they enter the overload set. | fixed | Boost.DateTime `libs/date_time/test//testdate`; Boost.Algorithm replacement storage constructs `process_segment_helper<has_stable_iterators<InputT>::value>` while `has_stable_iterators<std::string>::type` names `mpl::bool_<has_stable_iterators<std::string>::value>`. | Current-instantiation static member constants were blocked whenever the owner class template was still collecting/evaluating members, even when the `static const bool value` binding already held a concrete constant. That made the nested trait typedef fail while completing the same class template, and the enclosing class-template temporary call later fell through to function lookup as `unknown function process_segment_helper<...>`. The fix permits already-known constant/constexpr member values during owner collection, while preserving the incomplete guard for values that would require initializer evaluation and suppressing member-output/witness side effects during the reentrant read. | fixed | Boost.DateTime `libs/date_time/test` compile cluster in `testdate_input_facet`, `testparse_time`, `testtime_formatters`, `testtime_input_facet`, and both `testtime_serialize_versioning` variants; libc++ `std::pair<char const, boost::date_time::string_parse_tree<char>>` declares member `second` while `string_parse_tree<char>` is still reference-collecting a recursive map-like value type. | Class member collection rejected an object field as soon as its type lacked layout. That is too eager for class-template specializations used only as named value types during reference collection: the field can be recorded, while the containing class must remain incomplete until its field type's real layout is available. The fix records fields whose member type has a known class definition but deferred layout, and stops class population before final layout if any such fields remain. A true by-value recursion still fails when layout is demanded. No source-text reparse, fallback resolver, or STL special case is added. | `pa21:200` alias-template / class-template composition | New `pa21/tests/general/200-deferred-incomplete-member-layout-alias-value-type.t` | No-STL reducer failed before the fix with `unsupported class member object name=second type=struct tree<char>` while resolving `tree<char>::value_type`; clang accepts the reducer. DateTime failed the same shape in libc++ `pair.h:97`. | fixed | Boost.DateTime `libs/date_time/test//testtime_input_facet`; special-value parsing in `time_input_facet::get` catches `std::out_of_range` inside switch cases for `%Y`, `%m`, `%a`, and `%d` fallback parsing. | Host-EH call-site collection propagated entry stacks through only the final jump and at most one immediately preceding conditional jump in a machine block. Switch lowering emits a chain of conditional jumps followed by an unconditional default jump in one dispatch block, so the first switch target could open an `eh_try` with no entry-stack record. Its throwing call was emitted without a landing-pad/action call-site entry, and the host runtime treated the exception as uncaught. The fix records all conditional jump targets when discovering host-EH successors. | fixed | Boost.DateTime `libs/date_time/test//testtime_serialize_versioning_curr` and `//testtime_serialize_versioning_prev`; Boost.LexicalCast instantiates a polymorphic `basic_unlockedbuf<std::__1::basic_stringbuf<char, ...>, char>` while converting `std::ifstream::pos_type` to `std::string`. | The semantic-to-ABI type builder tried carried dependent template-template metadata before concrete class-template specialization mangle metadata. The fix lets concrete class-template specialization metadata with non-dependent arguments take the canonical ABI path before dependent template-template/class-template metadata. | fixed | Boost.Describe `libs/describe/test`; enum description macros use the GNU extension `, ##__VA_ARGS__` while expanding `BOOST_DESCRIBE_ENUM` / `BOOST_DEFINE_ENUM`. | Macro replacement treated `, ##__VA_ARGS__` as ordinary token concatenation. With non-empty varargs it tried to paste `,` with the first argument token, retokenized that spelling as two preprocessing tokens, and threw `Join resulted in more than one token`. The fix recognizes only the literal GNU variadic comma-paste form: empty `__VA_ARGS__` removes the comma, while non-empty `__VA_ARGS__` appends the raw variadic tokens without token pasting. Normal `##` behavior remains unchanged. | `pa4` macro replacement | New `cppgm.tests/course/pa4/301-gnu-vaargs-comma-paste.t` | The PA4 reducer and Boost `enumerators_test.cpp` / `trailing_comma_test.cpp` failed before the fix with `ERROR: Join resulted in more than one token`. | `make -C dev macro cppgm++ -j8`; focused PA4 reducer passes; `make test-pa4 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1` passes local PA4 `38/38` and course PA4 `31/31`; direct Boost compiles of `enumerators_test.cpp` and `trailing_comma_test.cpp` pass; direct `has_enumerators_test.cpp` advances to the remaining trait-call conversion frontier; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-pr36-3ee8fb3f8-20260630-perf-baseline.json` passes (`-0.16%` instructions, RSS `-0.44%`, footprint `-0.08%`). | this commit |

The second trait call could therefore reuse the first function-template instantiation and reject the function-pointer argument. The fix keeps class and variable template keys on the existing path, and only for function-template keys uses structured concrete class-template specialization metadata when the identity text still mentions the class-template parameter. | fixed | Boost.Exception `libs/exception/test//no_exceptions_test` and `//diagnostic_information_test`; `boost::diagnostic_information(std::exception const&)` uses `dynamic_cast` from a non-primary `std::exception` base back to `boost::exception` on a local multiply-inherited exception object. | Host-runtime secondary vtable slices for non-virtual bases omitted the Itanium vtable prefix. Constructors stored the non-primary base subobject vptr at the start of that slice, so host `dynamic_cast` read `vptr[-1]` as a function pointer instead of RTTI and crashed in libc++abi. The fix emits `offset-to-top` and RTTI for all host-runtime vtables and stores the secondary vptr at the ABI address point. | fixed | Boost.Exception `libs/exception/test//cloning_test` first runtime frontier; `throw boost::enable_current_exception(derives_nothing(count))` builds the exception object from a class prvalue returned by `enable_current_exception`, and the source temporary must be destroyed before the catch observes `count`. | Throw-statement lowering did not create a full-expression cleanup scope for the throw operand. When the operand was a class-returning call fed by a materialized class temporary, the temporary cleanup registered by call lowering was never emitted before `__cxa_throw`, leaving an extra live object during the catch. The fix scopes throw operands as full expressions and emits those cleanups after the exception object is initialized but before the host/private throw transfer. No source-text reparse, fallback resolver, or Boost special case is added. | `pa33:200` host EH runtime cleanup behavior | New `pa33/tests/general/200-host-eh-throw-return-temp-cleanup.t` | No-Boost reducer `throw make_wrapper(tracked(count))` failed before the fix with `during_catch == 2`; generated LowIR showed `tracked` construction and factory return into the exception object followed by `__cxa_throw` with no temporary destructor. Focused Boost `cloning_test` no longer reports the first `count==0` assertion and now advances to a later segfault. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; no-Boost factory-throw and Boost-shaped clone-count reducers pass; focused PA33 regression passes; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa33' ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `76/76`; PA33 placement audit reports `placement findings: 0`; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; focused B2 `/usr/local/bin/timeout 900 env JOBS=4 ... ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/exception/test//cloning_test` advances to the later segfault, log `/tmp/boost-exception-cloning-after-throw-operand-cleanup-20260701.log`; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-pr36-3ee8fb3f8-20260630-perf-baseline.json` passes (`+0.31%` instructions, RSS `+1.76%`, footprint `-0.12%`). | this commit |

| fixed | Boost.Exception `libs/exception/test//cloning_test` second runtime frontier; a thrown `may_throw_on_copy<std::bad_alloc>` is copied during `throw *this`, then the nested `boost::exception_ptr` is destroyed through the `clone_base` secondary vtable view. | Two issues were layered in this runtime path. Host EH throw-copy lowering classified the derived implicit copy constructor as trivial because the output/type-trait trivial-copy helpers ignored user-provided base copy constructors, and reused implicit constructor bindings were not marked as copy/move constructors. After the throwing copy ran, `clone_impl<bad_alloc_>` still reused a destructor thunk keyed only by target symbol; the `clone_base` view at offset 48 called the thunk built for the `std::bad_alloc` view at offset 40 and destroyed the object from the wrong `this`. The fix routes output trivial-copy checks through the class model, teaches the callsemantic trait helper about nontrivial copy constructors, normalizes reused implicit constructor bindings, and keys vtable-entry thunks by their adjustment tuple. No source-text reparse, fallback resolver, or Boost special case is added. | `pa33:200` host EH copy construction; `pa36:600` linked secondary-vtable thunk behavior | New `pa33/tests/general/200-host-eh-throw-copy-constructor-throws.t`; new `pa36/tests/link/600-secondary-vtable-delete-thunk-link-smoke.t` | The no-STL throw-copy reducer used `copyobj` instead of calling the throwing base copy constructor, and the Boost-shaped bad-allocation reducer segfaulted while destroying a nested `exception_ptr`. LowIR for the Boost reducer showed the `clone_base` vtable slice pointing at destructor thunks that adjusted by `-40` instead of `-48`. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; no-STL direct and throw-copy reducers exit `0`; Boost bad-allocation step3 and full bad-allocation reducers exit `0`; LowIR inspection shows distinct `this_neg40` and `this_neg48` destructor thunks, with the `clone_base` slice using `this_neg48`; focused PA33 and PA36 regressions pass; PA33/PA36 placement audit has `placement findings: 0`; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa33 pa36' ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `147/147`; focused Boost `cloning_test` advances to a separate invalid free in `clone_impl<derives_std_boost_exception>` during `boost::diagnostic_information`, log `/tmp/boost-exception-cloning-after-copy-and-thunk-fix-20260701.log`; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-pr36-3ee8fb3f8-20260630-perf-baseline.json` passes (`+0.06%` instructions, RSS `+0.39%`, footprint `-0.15%`). | this commit |
| fixed | Boost.Exception `libs/exception/test//cloning_test` diagnostic-information runtime frontier; `boost::diagnostic_information(exception_ptr)` calls `std::exception::what()` through the primary base of a thrown `clone_impl<derives_std_boost_exception>` object that also has a virtual base. | Primary vtables for classes with virtual bases used an internal extended slot stride, storing an extra `this`-adjust word after each function pointer. Host ABI callers, and cppgm calls through a base type that does not itself use virtual bases, index the dynamic vtable with the ordinary Itanium pointer stride. The `std::exception::what()` call therefore loaded the deleting destructor slot and freed the exception object. The fix keeps all vtable entries on the host ABI pointer stride and relies on vtable-entry thunks plus the existing virtual-base-offset prefix for adjustments. No source-text reparse, fallback resolver, or Boost special case is added. | `pa32:200` host-linkable virtual-base vtable layout | New `pa32/tests/general/200-primary-vtable-virtual-base-standard-stride.t` | The Boost-shaped diagnostic reducer `/tmp/cppgm_boost_exception_diag_min.cpp` aborted with `pointer being freed was not allocated`; generated LowIR showed `clone_impl<derives_std_boost_exception>` primary vtable entries as D1, adjustment, D0, adjustment, `what`, so the base virtual call at `vptr + 16` landed on D0. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; the Boost diagnostic reducer now exits `0` and its LowIR primary vtable is standard-stride D1, D0, `what`; focused PA32 regression passes; PA32 placement audit has `placement findings: 0`; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa32 pa36' ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `150/150`; focused Boost `cloning_test` passes when the wrapper is pointed at this worktree (`CPPGM_B2_CXX=/Users/vishvananda/cppgm-extended/dev/cppgm++`), log `/tmp/boost-exception-cloning-after-standard-vtable-stride-current-worktree-20260701.log`; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-pr36-3ee8fb3f8-20260630-perf-baseline.json` passes (`+0.14%` instructions, RSS `+0.35%`, footprint `-0.09%`). | this commit |

| fixed | Boost.Exception `libs/exception/test//throw_exception_test` link frontier; multiple `boost::wrapexcept<T>` specializations emitted non-virtual deleting-destructor thunks through a shared unresolved template spelling. | Host vtable thunk export naming rebuilt the target symbol from the vtable entry's unresolved qualified syntax. For template specializations that left names like `wrapexcept<T>::~wrapexcept` in the vtable entry syntax, distinct concrete thunks collapsed to the same raw Itanium symbol such as `_ZThn16_N5boost10wrapexceptIT_ED0Ev`. The fix derives exported thunk object names from the already-concrete target object symbol first, using the old qualified-name route only as a fallback. The fix distinguishes `T({...})` from `T{...}` using the structured token after the callee, preserves parenthesized braced lists as one target-aware argument, and keeps no-viable constructor probes as semantic soft failures. No source-text reparse, fallback resolver, or Boost special case is added. | `pa16:200` class value-semantics constructor argument initialization | New `pa16/tests/general/200-parenthesized-braced-aggregate-constructor-arg.t` | The no-STL reducer `Holder h({1, 2}); Holder h2 = Holder({3, 4});` failed before the fix with `no viable constructor [arg_count 2]`, while clang accepted it. | fixed | Boost.Exception `libs/exception/test//nlohmann_json_test` host RTTI/typeinfo-name frontier for `boost::exception_serialization::nlohmann_json_encoder<nlohmann::ordered_json>`, where `ordered_json` is an alias for `basic_json<nlohmann::ordered_map>`. | ABI symbol construction for class template specializations with template-template arguments derived the entity name from the mutable `TemplateArgument::template_decl`. The resolver had already identified the `ordered_map` template, but by late typeinfo emission that decl could have an empty name/scope, so host RTTI symbol construction failed. The fix carries the resolved template entity scope/name as structured `TemplateArgument` identity through resolution, binding, and deduction, and the symbol-linkage path consumes that identity before falling back to declaration state. No source-text reparse, fallback resolver, cache, or Boost special case is added. | `pa25:100` RTTI/typeid for class-template specializations with template-template arguments | New `pa25/tests/general/100-typeid-template-template-argument-typeinfo-name.t` | The no-STL PA25 reducer failed before the fix with the same host typeinfo-name construction failure for an alias-selected template-template argument. Focused Boost B2 `nlohmann_json_test` advances past the typeinfo failure to the next frontier: `unknown lvalue id-expression __args__pack2` while compiling the libc++ `std::__1::vector<...>::emplace_back` lambda operator. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused PA25 regression passes; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa25' ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `58/58`; PA25 placement audit reports no early-placement findings; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py` reports all zero; `git diff --check` passes; focused B2 `/usr/local/bin/timeout 900 env JOBS=4 ... ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/exception/test//nlohmann_json_test` advances to `__args__pack2`; one-run perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-pr36-3ee8fb3f8-20260630-perf-baseline.json` passes (`+0.51%` instructions, RSS `+1.54%`, footprint `+1.77%`; wall `55.550s -> 63.090s`). | this commit |

| fixed | Boost.Exception `libs/exception/test//nlohmann_json_test` libc++ `std::__1::vector<...>::emplace_back` lambda frontier; the lambda captures an enclosing forwarding parameter pack and forwards `__args...`, but LowIR reported `unknown lvalue id-expression __args__pack2`. | Implicit lambda capture collection saw the source spelling `__args` and captured only that first alias. The instantiated lambda body expanded the enclosing named value pack to aliases such as `__args__pack2`, while the closure member scope had no mirrored value-pack binding, so lambda-operator lowering tried to use an outer function parameter that was not local to the operator. The fix expands implicit captures of named value packs into their concrete `ValueBinding` aliases and mirrors fully captured packs into the closure member scope. No source-text reparse, fallback resolver, cache, or Boost special case is added. | `pa24:300` lambdas capturing enclosing forwarding parameter packs | New `pa24/tests/general/300-capturing-lambda-enclosing-pack-forward-call.t` | The checked-in no-STL reducer mirrors the libc++ shape with `[&] { return sink(std::forward<Args>(args)...); }` inside a function template. 2026-07-02 Boost.Xpressive `libs/xpressive/test/regress.cpp` constructor-template
partial-ordering frontier: `make_dynamic` constructs
`sequence<BidiIter>(xpr)` where `xpr` is an `intrusive_ptr` to a
`dynamic_xpression<alternate_matcher<alternates_vector<BidiIter>, Traits>,
BidiIter>`. The two viable `sequence` constructor templates instantiate to the
same concrete parameter type, but the narrower constructor keeps more concrete
nested template-id structure than the generic `Matcher` wrapper. The normal
bidirectional partial-order deduction path and existing placeholder-count
tie-breaker still tied, so overload resolution reported an ambiguous
constructor. The fix adds a structured template-id specificity tie-breaker that
scores semantic template argument structure after ordinary partial ordering and
the existing local tie-breakers tie; it uses carried type/template argument data
and adds no source-text reparse or Boost special case. Owner: `pa22:200`
function-template partial ordering. New regression:
`pa22/tests/spec/200-constructor-template-qualified-nested-id-partial-ordering.t`.
Pre-fix evidence: the namespace-qualified reducer and direct Boost
`regress.cpp` compile failed with the ambiguous
`boost::xpressive::detail::sequence<...>` constructor diagnostic. After the fix,
the reducer passes, direct Boost `regress.cpp` produces
`/tmp/boost-xpressive-regress-current.o`, and a focused B2
`libs/xpressive/test` run produces `regress.o` before moving on to unrelated
Boost.Test dependency rebuilds. Validation: `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused
PA22 and existing PA23 partial-ordering guards pass; direct-LowIR PA22 report
passes `182/182`; full direct-LowIR `test-report-nobuild` passes `3422/3422`;
full direct-LowIR strict suite passes; PA22 placement audit reports no
early-placement findings; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py --strict`
reports all zero; `git diff --check` passes. | this commit |

2026-07-02 Boost.Xpressive `libs/xpressive/test/test3.cpp` `make_adaptor`
frontier: argument analysis for `make_adaptor<matchable_ex<BidiIter>>`
instantiates Proto `reverse_fold`/`use_simple_repeat` machinery and reaches a
compiler-generated unsigned non-type value spelling `(unsigned int)1048578ULL`.
The legacy NTTP text evaluator already accepted simple integer literals and
`static_cast` forms, but not this synthetic C-style cast form, so overload
probing dropped the valid adaptor candidate and reported `unknown function
make_adaptor<matchable_ex<BidiIter>>`. The fix adds a narrow evaluator for
C-style casts to fundamental integral type spellings in the existing integral
text path; it adds no source-text reparse, fallback resolver, cache, or Boost
special case. Owner: `pa34:500` hosted compiler-intrinsic compatibility because
the compact reducer exercises `__make_integer_seq` count evaluation through the
hosted intrinsic surface; the implementation path is the shared NTTP evaluator.
New regression: `pa34/tests/compile/500-make-integer-seq-cstyle-cast-count.t`.
Pre-fix evidence: the no-STL `__make_integer_seq<integer_sequence, unsigned,
(unsigned int)3ULL>` reducer failed with `legacy non-type template argument text
evaluation: (unsigned int)3ULL`; the small Xpressive header probe and direct
Boost `test3.cpp` compile failed with the `make_adaptor` diagnostic. After the
fix, the reducer, the header probe, and direct Boost `test3.cpp` compile all
pass. A focused B2 `libs/xpressive/test` rerun no longer contains the
`make_adaptor` or legacy non-type diagnostics, but still exits `1` on later
frontiers: Boost.Test dependency `framework.o` reports `unknown function
call_impl`, and several Xpressive targets report failure to build an ABI IR
symbol for weak `boost::proto::exprns_::extends<...>::extends` while populating
`basic_regex<BidiIter>::ECMAScript`. Validation: `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`;
focused PA34 regression passes; PA34 direct-LowIR report passes `885/885`; PA34
placement audit reports no early-placement findings; `python3
scripts/audit_text_reparse.py --strict` reports all zero; full direct-LowIR
strict suite passes; direct Boost `test3.cpp` produces
`/tmp/boost-xpressive-test3-after-cstyle.o`; `git diff --check` passes. | this
commit |

2026-07-02 Boost.Xpressive `libs/xpressive/test/test5.cpp` and `test5u.cpp`
member-template NTTP shadowing frontier: `static_xpression::get_width()` calls
`this->get_width_(mpl::size_t<width>())`, where `width` is the instantiated
class value `3`, while the selected member-template overload has a non-type
template parameter named `Width`. Source signature collection already let that
member-template parameter shadow inherited values, but instantiated parameter-
clause refresh reparsed `mpl::size_t<Width>` in a temporary scope whose
`class_info` lookup saw the inherited `base_value<0>::Width` before the parent
function-template binding `Width=3`. The refreshed overload parameter became
`mpl::size_t<0>`, so overload resolution rejected the exact `mpl::size_t<3>`
call. The fix seeds instantiated parameter-clause scopes with only referenced
template-bound names as direct bindings before class member lookup, and also
overlays template-bound names into instantiated function body scopes before
body statements are analyzed. This preserves template-parameter shadowing
without source reparsing or fallback resolution. Owner: `pa22:200` member
function-template deduction/signature refresh and instantiated body scope
materialization. New regression:
`pa22/tests/spec/200-member-template-nontype-param-shadows-inherited-value-sum.t`.
Pre-fix evidence: the no-STL reducer failed with `no viable overload for member
call get_width_`, and the trace showed deduction binding `Width=3` followed by
parameter refresh resolving `mpl_::size_t<0>`; after the parameter-clause fix,
the same reducer compiled but lowered `result(Width)` in the function body as
`result(0)`, matching the Xpressive runtime assertion. Validation: `make -C dev
cppgm++ -j8`; direct reducer emits `result(3)` and exits `0`; focused PA22 old
and new shadowing reducers pass; PA22 direct-LowIR report passes `183/183`;
serialized full direct-LowIR report passes `3424/3424`; strict direct-LowIR
suite passes with the long-template timeout knobs `CPPGM_TEXT_TEST_TIMEOUT_SEC=60`
and `CPPGM_BATCH_TIMEOUT_SEC=60` (the PA23 static-query witness reducer is about
13.5s with or without this fix); PA22 placement audit passes; text-reparse audit
reports all zero; `git diff --check` passes. Focused B2 `test5` and `test5u`
both pass end to end with `CPPGM_B2_CXX=/Users/vishvananda/cppgm-extended/dev/cppgm++`.

2026-07-02 Boost.Xpressive `libs/xpressive/test//test_format` surrogate-call
frontier: after the `test10` guard, a no-force Xpressive continuation reached
`test_format.cpp`, where `formatter_arity<formatter_function*, ...>` incorrectly
classified a unary formatter as the zero-argument fallback. The real conversion
operator lives in the partial specialization `formatter_wrapper<Formatter *>`;
conversion-operator result substitution was still using the primary template
argument list, so `Formatter` was rebound as the pointer type instead of the
partial specialization's function-type binding. The inherited dummy conversion
operators then won surrogate-call overload resolution and Xpressive routed the
function pointer through the string-format path. The fix substitutes conversion
operator type-id syntax with the selected partial specialization's template
parameters and `ClassInfo::instantiation_binding_arguments` when present,
falling back to the primary binding for primary instantiations. No source-text
reparse, fallback resolver, cache, or Boost special case is added. Owner:
`pa21:400` class-template partial-specialization member substitution. New
regression:
`pa21/tests/general/400-partial-specialization-conversion-operator-pointer-binding.t`.
Pre-fix evidence: the no-STL reducer and direct Boost `test_format.cpp` compile
failed by treating `ForwardIterator` as a pointer-to-function formatter instead
of a string iterator path; tracing showed the partial specialization's
`operator Formatter*()` parsed but substituted with the primary `Formatter =
formatter_function*` argument. After the fix, the reducer passes, PA21 direct
LowIR report passes `184/184`, direct Boost `test_format.cpp` produces
`/tmp/boost-xpressive-test_format-fixed.o`, text-reparse audit reports all zero,
strict direct-LowIR witness suite passes, full direct-LowIR
`test-report-nobuild` passes `3425/3425`, and `git diff --check` passes. The
long-lived active baseline at
`/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-pr36-3ee8fb3f8-20260630-perf-baseline.json` misses the
cumulative dirty-tree instruction gate at `+1.81%`, while this fix isolated
against `/tmp/cppgm-before-partial-conversion-operator-binding-baseline.json`
passes three-run perf with instructions `+0.21%`, RSS `-0.70%`, footprint
`-0.03%`; report:
`/tmp/cppgm-perf-after-partial-conversion-operator-binding-isolated.json`.

2026-07-03 Boost.Xpressive `libs/xpressive/test//test_actions` Proto callable
transform frontier: `to_vector((s1,s2))` instantiates nested function-type
template arguments such as `push(get_value(value))`. Parameter declarations in
function-type template arguments tried the parenthesized declarator-name parse
before the function-style abstract declarator parse, so `get_value(value)` lost
the known `value` type and collapsed to `get_value`. Proto then deduced
`A0=get_value` instead of `A0=get_value (*)(value)`, and later calls through
`when<_, A0>::impl` failed as unknown functions. The fix prefers a
function-style abstract declarator when a parenthesized parameter tail can be
parsed that way, matching Clang for `A(B)` when `B` names a type. Owner:
`pa21:400` class partial-specialization matching of function-type template
arguments. New regression:
`pa21/tests/general/400-nested-function-type-argument-partial-specialization.t`.
Pre-fix evidence: the no-STL Proto-shaped reducer failed with `unknown function
typename when<_,A0>::template impl<Expr,State,Data>` and traced `A0` as
`get_value`; after the parser fix, the new PA21 witness records
`specialize #2 = get_value (*)(value)`. Temporary Proto-specific
`template_argument_semantics`, `semantic_overload`, and `callsemantic` edits
from the paused stash were checked in a detached parser-only worktree and found
unnecessary: parser-only passed the Proto reducer, the direct Xpressive
`to_vector` reducer, the new PA21 regression, and focused B2
`libs/xpressive/test//test_actions`, so those stale edits were dropped. The
focused B2 run with the cleaned main worktree also passes and updates 41
targets; `test_actions.cpp` remains an expensive compile, taking several
minutes at one core and peaking around 2.7 GB RSS, with sample
`/tmp/cppgm-xpressive-test-actions.sample.txt` showing normal template semantic
analysis. Validation: `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused Proto and Xpressive
reducers compile; focused PA21 regression passes; PA21 direct-LowIR report
passes `185/185`; PA21 placement audit exits cleanly; `python3
scripts/audit_text_reparse.py --strict` reports all zero; full direct-LowIR
`test-report-nobuild` passes `3426/3426`; strict direct-LowIR witness suite
passes.

2026-07-03 Boost.Xpressive `libs/xpressive/test//test_non_char` hosted
`char_traits` frontier: after the `test_actions` fix, a no-force Xpressive
continuation reached `test_non_char.cpp`, where Boost.Xpressive's `UChar`
iterator path asks for `std::char_traits<UChar>::int_type`, then casts an
integer through `(Char)i`, then calls
`std::char_traits<UChar>::to_char_type` and `to_int_type`. On the hosted libc++
lane the `char_traits` primary is undefined, but this Boost path still expects
the compiler compatibility surface to treat the primary like a narrow integer
conversion shim. The fix extends class-object direct casts so C-style
class-object casts reuse the existing functional/direct-initialization path,
and adds a hosted compatibility hook only for qualified `std::char_traits<T>`
`int_type`, `to_char_type`, and `to_int_type` when lookup resolves the declaring
template in namespace `std`. The shim maps `int_type` to `int`; the two
conversion calls still analyze their single argument through normal target
expression analysis and do not emit an unresolved library call. Owners:
`pa16:200` C-style class-object direct initialization and `pa34:700` hosted
`char_traits` primary compatibility. New regressions:
`pa16/tests/general/200-cstyle-class-cast-direct-initializes.t` and
`pa34/tests/compile/700-hosted-char-traits-primary-conversion-shims.t`.
Pre-fix evidence: direct `test_non_char.cpp` first failed resolving
`std::char_traits<UChar>::int_type`, then failed `(Char)i` as a built-in cast,
then failed the `to_char_type` member call. Validation: `make -C dev cppgm++
-j8`; compact C-style class-cast and `char_traits` reducers compile; focused
PA16 and PA34 owner checks pass; PA16/PA34 direct-LowIR report passes
`411/411`; PA16 and PA34 placement audits report zero placement findings and
zero local hygiene findings; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py --strict`
reports all zero; focused B2
`libs/xpressive/test//test_non_char` passes and updates 41 targets; strict
direct-LowIR witness suite passes; full direct-LowIR `test-report-nobuild`

2026-07-03 Boost.Xpressive `libs/xpressive/test//test_symbols` elaborated
type-scope frontier: after the `test_non_char` fix, a no-force Xpressive
continuation built and ran `test_symbols` but failed at runtime by replaying
symbol-table semantic actions during skipped/repeated static-regex matching
(`"foo"` produced `11`, and the full test produced long duplicated action
traces). Instrumenting a shadow copy of Xpressive showed the action queue held
one action; the extra execution came from `keep(*_s)` selecting
`independent_end_xpression` instead of `true_xpression` for Boost.Proto's
`NotHasAction` grammar. The root cause was scope introduction for unqualified
elaborated type specifiers: in `struct NotHasAction :
proto::switch_<struct NotHasActionCases> {};`, cppgm++ introduced
`NotHasAction::NotHasActionCases`, while Clang introduces the forward
declaration in the enclosing namespace, matching the later namespace-scope
definition. The fix changes elaborated forward-type introduction to skip
class member scopes for unqualified names and declare in the nearest enclosing
namespace/block scope, while leaving ordinary class definitions unchanged.
Owner: `pa23:500` template argument elaborated type scope. New regression:
`pa23/tests/general/500-elaborated-template-argument-enclosing-scope.t`.
Pre-fix evidence: the compile-time reducer selected
`independent_end_xpression=1` for `decltype(*boost::xpressive::_s)` and direct
`boost::proto::matches<terminal, NotHasAction>` failed with an incomplete
nested `NotHasAction::NotHasActionCases` base; runtime reducers showed
`skip(_s)(+(a1 = map)[result += a1])` produced `11` for `"foo"`. After the fix,
the type reducer selects `true_xpression=1`, the Proto `matches` reducer
passes, the one-token and full-symbol reducers produce the expected strings,
PA23 direct-LowIR report passes `379/379`, focused B2
`libs/xpressive/test//test_symbols` passes and updates 41 targets, and the
no-force `libs/xpressive/test` continuation passes all updated Xpressive
targets. The focused `test_symbols.cpp` compile remains expensive; samples
`/tmp/cppgm-boost-test_symbols-compile.sample.txt` and
`/tmp/cppgm-boost-test_symbols-compile-2.sample.txt` show template semantic
output/name-mangling and nested template instantiation work rather than a
single tight spin.

2026-07-03 Boost.Flyweight `libs/flyweight/test` default pointer NTTP frontier:
the Flyweight factory specifier declarations use default non-type template
arguments such as `(boost::flyweights::detail::not_a_ph_expr*)0`. The default
argument was present in the merged template declaration, but non-type argument
evaluation only handled the integral `0` case and did not evaluate a C-style
cast of that zero to the pointer parameter type. The fix recognizes structured
C-style null pointer casts for pointer/member-pointer non-type template
parameters and records the template argument value as `0`; it does not add
source-text fallback or a Boost special case. Owner: `pa22:400` pointer/reference
non-type template argument evaluation. New regression:
`pa22/tests/general/400-defaulted-pointer-nontype-cstyle-null.t`. Pre-fix
evidence: the reducer and the full Flyweight suite failed with
`failed default non-type template argument evaluation`, and the diagnostic text
was blank because the expression renderer did not print the cast expression.
Validation: `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused reducer compile passes; focused
PA22 owner check passes; PA19 direct-LowIR report passes `122/122`; PA22
direct-LowIR report passes `184/184`; full direct-LowIR report passes
`3430/3430`; default strict witness suite passes; PA22 placement audit reports
no early-placement findings; `python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py --strict`
reports all zero; `git diff --check` passes. The long-lived active baseline at
`/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-pr36-3ee8fb3f8-20260630-perf-baseline.json` still
misses cumulative instruction tolerance at `+2.07%`; isolated against clean
`da1f34d7` baseline `/tmp/cppgm-before-flyweight-nullptr-baseline.json`, this
fix passes three-run perf with instructions `-0.14%`, RSS `-0.03%`, footprint
`+0.01%`. Focused B2 `libs/flyweight/test//test_basic` advances past the
default NTTP failure to the next Boost.Parameter duplicate-class frontier at
`boost/parameter/aux_/arg_list.hpp:379`, log
`/tmp/boost-flyweight-test-basic-after-nullptr-cast-20260703.log`.

2026-07-03 Boost.Flyweight `libs/flyweight/test//test_basic` member-typedef
qualifier frontier: `flyweight<T>` aliases its instantiated implementation as
`typedef detail::flyweight_core<T> core`, while namespace `boost::core` is also
visible. Constructor analysis for `h(core::insert(...))` resolved the leading
qualifier as the namespace first, so ordinary function lookup reported
`unknown function core::insert` against `boost::core` instead of looking in the
instantiated `flyweight_core` member scope. The fix makes unrooted qualifier
resolution honor the ordinary lexical lookup order at each scope level, so a
current-instantiation member typedef can hide an outer namespace, and it keeps
source-template member function bindings when they belong to a concrete
class-template instantiation. Node-qualified function lookup now reuses the
resolved class/namespace scope for simple qualifiers; structured qualifier
syntax stays on the existing path to preserve witness source-use behavior. No
source-text fallback, cache bypass, or Boost special case is added. Owner:
`pa18:300` class-template member typedef/current-instantiation qualified
lookup. New regression:
`pa18/tests/general/300-member-typedef-qualifier-hides-outer-namespace.t`.
Pre-fix evidence: the no-STL reducer failed with `unknown function core::insert`
and the direct Boost object failed at the same lookup frontier. Validation:
`make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused reducer and PA18 regression pass; PA18
direct-LowIR report passes `195/195`; PA18 placement audit reports no
early-placement findings; default strict witness suite passes; full
direct-LowIR report passes `3432/3432`; `python3
scripts/audit_text_reparse.py --strict` reports all zero; `git diff --check`
passes; focused direct Boost object compile advances to the next
`insert_rep`/`unsupported enumerator value` frontier, log
`/tmp/boost-flyweight-test-basic-direct-after-qualified-node2-20260703.log`;
isolated perf against clean `3af8cc787` baseline
`/tmp/cppgm-before-flyweight-qualified-baseline.json` passes three-run perf with
instructions `+0.17%`, RSS `+0.63%`, footprint `-0.00%`.

2026-07-03 Boost.Flyweight `libs/flyweight/test//test_concurrent_factory`
lambda closure assignment frontier: after the object-pointer NTTP fixes, the
focused target reached libc++ `std::tuple<std::unique_ptr<int>, lambda>`
construction while completing the concurrent factory task wrapper. The
compiler synthesized implicit copy/move assignment operators for lambda closure
classes, so libc++ assignment SFINAE treated the closure as assignable and
forced ABI symbol construction for a weak lambda `operator=`. C++11 closure
types do not have those assignment operators. The fix makes implicit copy and
move assignment synthesis return no binding for lambda closure class metadata;
no libc++ or Boost special case is added. Owner: `pa24:200` lambda closure
semantics. New regression:
`pa24/tests/general/200-lambda-closure-assignment-sfinae.t`. Pre-fix evidence:
the focused Boost object compile failed during tuple/unique_ptr construction,
and reduced hosted tuple/unique_ptr and no-STL SFINAE probes reproduced the
same assignability shape. Validation: `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; direct
reducers pass; focused PA24 check passes; PA24 placement audit reports no
early-placement findings; focused direct Boost object compile advances through
the lambda assignment compile frontier, log
`/tmp/boost-flyweight-test-concurrent-direct-after-lambda-assign-20260703.log`.
The following focused B2 run linked but aborted at runtime with `std::bad_alloc`,
which reduced to the static constexpr floating global output frontier below.

2026-07-03 Boost.Flyweight `libs/flyweight/test//test_concurrent_factory`
static constexpr floating global frontier: Boost.Unordered's concurrent node
set uses a namespace-scope `static constexpr float mlf = 0.875f`. When that
object was referenced, semantic output folded the constant initializer through
the integral constexpr conversion helper and emitted `global ... = 0`, so
runtime growth divided by zero and requested an enormous allocation. The fix
keeps the existing integral/enum path but emits typed floating constexpr
literal nodes for floating object definitions, preserving float and long-double
suffixes in LowIR text. Owner: `pa20:400` constexpr floating/static storage.

the Boost concurrent-node-set and Flyweight concurrent-factory reducers aborted
with `std::bad_alloc`. Validation: `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; the LowIR dump
for the reducer now contains `global @ns__mlf : f32 ... = 0.875f`; focused

GNU legacy sync builtin frontier: Boost.Interprocess's atomic helper layer uses
`__sync_synchronize`, `__sync_fetch_and_add`, and
`__sync_val_compare_and_swap` from `boost/interprocess/detail/atomic.hpp` while
building `test_intermod_holder`. The existing hosted builtin support covered
only `__sync_lock_test_and_set` and `__sync_lock_release`, so the focused
Flyweight target stopped at `unknown function __sync_synchronize`. The fix
advertises the three Interprocess sync builtins through `__has_builtin`, analyzes
them with the existing atomic pointer/value helpers, lowers the full fence to
`atomic_thread_fence 5`, lowers fetch-add through `atomic_add_fetch` while
returning the old value, and lowers compare-and-swap through the existing LowIR
`atomic_compare_exchange` expected-pointer contract so it returns the prior
memory value. Owner: `pa34` hosted GNU builtin compatibility, matching the
earlier sync-lock builtin coverage. New regression:
`pa34/tests/compile/600-gnu-sync-interprocess-builtins.t`. Pre-fix evidence:
clean `1bb1bfef7` fails the reducer with `#error expected
__sync_synchronize`; Clang accepts the reducer and the patched reducer
executable exits `0`. Validation: `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused PA34
reducer passes; direct reducer executable exits `0`; PA34 direct-LowIR report
passes `276/276`; PA34 placement audit has no failing findings; full
direct-LowIR report passes `3439/3439`; full strict direct-LowIR compare passes;
`python3 scripts/audit_text_reparse.py --strict` reports all zero; `git diff
--check` passes; focused B2
`/usr/local/bin/timeout 1200 env JOBS=4 CPPGM_BOOST_B2_FRONTIER=1 ... ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/flyweight/test//test_intermod_holder`
advances both `intermod_holder_dll.o` and `test_intermod_holder.o` to `failed to
materialize lazy function body: expected compound-statement fragment near
OP_LBRACE:{` while emitting
`boost::interprocess::segment_manager<...>::priv_generic_named_construct`, log
`/tmp/boost-flyweight-test-intermod-holder-after-sync-builtins-20260703.log`;

`/tmp/cppgm-before-sync-builtins-baseline.json` passes with instructions

parameter named `name`, so the replay parser treated the member-template name as
a local value-name collision and left `<CharT>` as an expression operator. The
fix has member access consume the optional `.template` / `->template`
disambiguator before qualified-name parsing, preserves the textual `template `
marker on the AST identifier for dependent-member validation, and lets an
explicit template prefix override the qualified-name parser's value-name
template-suffix suppression. Owner: PA34 lazy hosted header-body replay, even
though the language construct itself is PA21 member-template syntax. New
regression: `pa34/tests/compile/700-lazy-header-member-template-shadow.t`.

Boost.Intrusive defaulted rebind constructor-deduction frontier:

deduction compared the pattern

normalization could not prove that the named namespace-scope constant default
matched the concrete value, so the viable pointer-conversion constructor was

special case is added. Owner: `pa22:500` constructor-template deduction through
defaulted class-template arguments and non-type defaults. New regression:
`pa22/tests/spec/500-defaulted-rebind-constructor-deduction.t`. Pre-fix
evidence: clean `e80df834d` fails the checked-in reducer and the Boost-shaped

2026-07-03 Boost.Flyweight `libs/flyweight/test//test_intermod_holder`
enum-member trivial-destructor frontier: after the defaulted rebind fix, the
focused target reached a dynamic-library link failure from
`boost::interprocess::ec_xlate` static table cleanup: LowIR emitted an internal

definition. The reduced no-STL shape is a `static const` aggregate class array
with an enum member; enum-valued aggregate initialization forced runtime
construction, while the output/lifetime trivial-destructor predicates treated

recognizes named enum types as trivially destructible before class metadata
lookup in semantic lifetime and output. Owner: PA15 global class-array lifetime
lowering. New regression:

creates a spurious cleanup-only call site; dedicated PA31 cleanup tests still
cover real cleanup facts. Pre-fix evidence: clean `8741ab159` emits
`declare function @Entry___Entry` and two `__cppgm_fini` calls for the PA15
reducer, and the Boost `ec_xlate` header probe fails to link with the same
undefined internal destructor. Validation: `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused
PA15 regression passes; PA15 direct-LowIR report passes `170/170`; PA15
placement audit reports no placement findings; focused PA31 compact-unwind
